# Snap-in Catalog and Porting Protocol

**Status and authority.** This document is drawn from `CONTRACT.md` and states nothing `CONTRACT.md` has not already frozen; where a catalog entry exposes something `CONTRACT.md` does not settle, the defect is recorded in `## §R` and repaired in place in `CONTRACT.md`, never stated as a rule here — per `CONTEXT D-07` and Phase 3's `CONTEXT D-12`. Where a `## §R` row and the clause it names disagree, the clause governs. [docs-verified] [CONTEXT D-07] [CONTRACT §Conventions]

**The three deep modalities are projected, not restated (D-04).** LightRAG, HippoRAG 2 and codebase-memory-mcp carry full master-table rows and thin entries whose fields cite `PARTS.md ## §L` / `## §H` / `## §X`; `PARTS.md` governs their fields, this document governs the breadth entries. A divergence between a projected row and its `PARTS.md` source is a defect recorded in `## §R` like any other. [docs-verified] [CONTEXT D-04]

**Framing, carried from Phases 1–3.** An advantage not retained at an entry rung reads as **not yet earned at this rung**, never as a permanent limitation — the two advantage fields are `§6`'s decomposition ratchet made per-system, which is the unification claim (owner priority #6) stated concretely. [inference] [CONTRACT §6] [CONTEXT D-09]

## Reading order

- A **snap-in answerer** starts at `## §T`.
- A **porter** starts at the system's `## §E` entry, then `## §C`.
- A **repair auditor** starts at `## §R`.
- A **coverage auditor** starts at `## Appendix A`.

## Conventions

This document reuses `CONTRACT.md`'s Conventions section wholesale by reference — RFC 2119, the four-value claim-tag vocabulary (`[code-verified]` / `[docs-verified]` / `[paper-claim]` / `[inference]`), the tagging rule — rather than redefining any of it, per D-04's "states nothing `CONTRACT.md` has not already frozen." [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §Conventions]

**Trace tags used in this document.** `[CONTRACT §N]` for contract clauses; `[PARTS §L]` / `[PARTS §H]` / `[PARTS §X]` / `[PARTS §P]` / `[PARTS §R]` for the projected deep three and PARTS.md's own register; `[CONTEXT D-NN]` for this phase's own locked decisions; `[SELECTION §…]` for D-VARIANTS/SELECTION.md; and `[MODALITIES-GRAPH §N]` / `[MODALITIES-VECTOR-TREE §N]` / `[MODALITIES-AGENTIC §N]` / `[MODALITIES-CODE-AND-ALIEN §N]` / `[GAP-SWEEP §N]` for breadth-entry evidence, per D-05's inherit-the-source's-own-tag rule.

**The entry shape.** Every per-system entry a later plan writes in `## §E` carries one field per line, each field name at column 1 so it is machine-checkable. These are exactly PARTS-06's six classification items carried as named fields, plus the four-bucket porting evidence and the two advantage fields:

- **`Entry path:`** — one of `decomposed wiring` / `partial with opaque core` / `whole-engine opaque node` / `external-tool`, or `out of scope — <CONTRACT clause>` per D-11.
- **`Node kinds:`** — `CONTRACT.md §13.4` types the system's nodes are typed against, or `opaque — one node`.
- **`Declared capabilities:`** — the `§14.2`/`§14.3`/`§14.4` members this system's nodes actually declare.
- **`Artifact scopes:`** — the `§3` scopes (`shared` / `quarantined` / `self_storage`) this system's nodes actually write.
- **`Port cost class:`** — one of `## §V`'s four named classes (`rehost` / `rewire` / `recut` / `rebuild`).
- **`Buckets (discard/replace/recut/carry over):`** — the four-bucket carry-over classification, quoted per `## §V`'s own quoted definitions, one clause per bucket.
- **`Advantages retained at entry:`** — the `## §V` advantage ids this system retains at the entry rung chosen, each marked `retained` with a one-line reason.
- **`Advantages decomposition would earn:`** — the `## §V` advantage ids a deeper cut would additionally buy, each marked `earned` with a one-line reason, per `CONTEXT D-09`'s "not yet earned" framing.
- **`Evidence:`** — the inherited claim tag plus source citation, per D-05.

**The no-blank-field rule.** A field with no members is written `none — <reason>`; an entry whose `Entry path:` reads `out of scope — CONTRACT §N` writes `n-a — out of scope` in every remaining field; no field is ever left empty and no field is closed by silence. [inference] [CONTEXT D-11]

**The entry-id convention.** Ids are derived from the survey source, never minted — `GR-n` for `MODALITIES-GRAPH.md ## n`, `VT-n` for `MODALITIES-VECTOR-TREE.md ## n`, `AG-n` for `MODALITIES-AGENTIC.md ## n`, `CA-n` for `MODALITIES-CODE-AND-ALIEN.md`'s numbered systems, `MC-1`/`MC-2` for postgres-mcp / supabase-mcp (whose entry path `CONTRACT §17` already settles), and `GS-Nn` keyed by the `GAP-SWEEP.md ## 2` verdict-table N-id. [inference] [CONTEXT D-10]

## §V — Vocabulary

**Satisfies:** PARTS-06

### The advantage vocabulary (D-08)

A **closed** list of ids `ADV-1` … `ADV-6`, derived from `CONTRACT.md` and `D-VARIANTS/SELECTION.md`, every member carrying its own claim tag and a citation to the clause that grounds it. The list is closed: an entry marks every member `retained` / `earned` / `n-a` with a one-line reason and may not introduce a seventh advantage in a cell. Started from CONTEXT D-08's candidate set (rig comparability, budget enforcement, trace attribution, artifact sharing, swap-invariance at the seam, versioning/promote-rollback) and kept all six — the evidence in each grounding clause below supports each candidate as stated, none merges with another and none drops for lack of a clause. [inference] [CONTEXT D-08]

| ADV id | Name | Grounding clause | Derivation |
|---|---|---|---|
| ADV-1 | Rig comparability | `CONTRACT §5` | `§5` specifies one gate implementation, not one per part or modality, with a fixed verb ladder (`check`/`preview`/`run`/`promote-next`/`promote-now`) — any node reachable through that gate is comparable to any other under the same A/A band, which is the comparability this advantage names. [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §5] |
| ADV-2 | Budget enforcement | `CONTRACT §9` | `§9` makes budget a splittable capability token, multiplicative under fan-out and apportioned at the merge side (D4) — every node inside a wiring inherits this enforcement regardless of how deep it is cut, which is the advantage this id names. [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §9] |
| ADV-3 | Trace attribution | `CONTRACT §4` | `§4`'s `provenance` is machine-stamped at the socket boundary and set-valued (D9) — a node inside the machine's `deps` graph gets this attribution automatically; an opaque node's internals do not. [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §4] |
| ADV-4 | Artifact sharing | `CONTRACT §3` | `§3`'s blast-radius rule gates `shared`-scope writes on effective depth `stage` — a node earns SA-1-shareable artifacts only once it (and everything it depends on) clears `opaque`/`quarantined`, which is exactly the ratchet this advantage tracks. [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §3] |
| ADV-5 | Swap-invariance at the seam | `CONTRACT §18.5` | `§18.5`'s tool-surface-growth rule and `§18.3`'s ownership clause together keep a modality swap invisible at the public seam — a node presenting the closed `§18.2` envelope earns this regardless of its own depth. [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §18.5] [CONTRACT §18.3] |
| ADV-6 | Versioning / promote-rollback | `CONTRACT §6` | `§6`'s node→subgraph promotion, ledger-wins-on-disagreement rule and tombstoning discipline apply to every registered component, decomposed or opaque, per `name@version` at `§0`. [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §6] |

### The port-cost class scale (D-12)

Four named ordinal classes, ordered cheapest to costliest, each anchored to which of the four buckets dominates a system's classification:

| Class | Dominant bucket | What it means |
|---|---|---|
| `rehost` | `carry over` | The system is hosted largely as-is; almost nothing is discarded, replaced or recut. Cheapest — the port is admission, not decomposition. |
| `rewire` | `replace` | The system's own orchestration shape survives; its store and client calls are rewired to the machine's own clients and stores. |
| `recut` | `recut` | The system's orchestration is decomposed into primitive-part nodes; a carried-over core (prompts, algorithmic internals) typically remains inside those nodes. |
| `rebuild` | `discard` | Most of the incumbent's own approach has no home in any other bucket and is thrown away; little to nothing carries over, and most of the port is fresh authoring. Costliest. |

This mapping is this document's own synthesis, not restated from a prior source: PARTS.md `## §P` states the buckets and states the "same class, still two rows" rule but does not itself name which bucket anchors which class name — that assignment is this section's job, and it is stated once here rather than re-derived per entry. [inference] [CONTEXT D-12]

Carrying `## §P`'s "same class, still two rows" rule forward verbatim in force: the class summarises the buckets and is never a merge key — two systems landing in the same class remain two entries with their own evidence. [docs-verified] [PARTS §P]

**Calibration against `PARTS.md ## §P`'s three modalities**, each mapping citing its own `## §P` row:

- **LightRAG** — `## §P` states its class as "re-cut, not a transcription": seventeen of eighteen query-side positions recut into primitive-part nodes over an opaque, carried-over ingest core. Dominant bucket: `recut`. Maps to `recut`. [docs-verified] [PARTS §P]
- **HippoRAG 2** — `## §P` states its class as "recut with a carried-over algorithmic core": all thirteen positions recut, with the PPR call and the OpenIE/reset-vector formulas carried over as internals. Dominant bucket: `recut`. Maps to `recut` — the same class as LightRAG, two rows, per the rule above. [docs-verified] [PARTS §P]
- **codebase-memory-mcp** — `## §P` states its class as "admission cost, not recut cost": nothing is discarded, replaced or recut; the whole engine carries over wholesale as the opaque node's internals. Dominant bucket: `carry over`. Maps to `rehost`. [docs-verified] [PARTS §P]

No modality on `## §P`'s record anchors `rewire` or `rebuild`; both classes remain defined by their dominant-bucket rule above and available for a breadth entry whose evidence lands there.

### The four buckets, quoted rather than re-invented

Quoted from `REQUIREMENTS.md`'s own `PARTS-07` wording, corrected here to the byte-exact source after this plan's own reconciliation found both this quotation and `PARTS.md ## §P`'s closing quotation had drifted in the same direction (each inserted "the" before "machine already owns it," a word `REQUIREMENTS.md:56` does not carry): *discard* — "machine already owns it"; *replace* — "rewire to machine clients and stores"; *recut* — "orchestration into primitive-part nodes"; *carry over* — "prompts, ontologies, algorithmic cores as component internals." [docs-verified] [PARTS-07]

### AG-2 — CRAG (Corrective RAG)

Entry path: `decomposed wiring`, no loop anywhere — `MODALITIES-AGENTIC.md ## 2`'s own framing ("linear pipeline with a three-way conditional branch; no loop, no recursion") reduces directly to two nested `## §19.8` data guards rather than any harness-tier machinery: the first guard tests "correct vs. not," the second tests "incorrect vs. ambiguous," both evaluated against the same grader node's own threshold scores, each declared with its evaluating node, its default emitted socket value on the non-firing branch, and per-query granularity per `## §19.8`'s guard-granularity rule. Unlike `AG-1`, whose grader-evaluated re-entry can escalate to a genuine harness-tier long-form loop, CRAG's "re-entry on a verdict" never loops back to the same retrieval — the incorrect branch dispatches to a *different* retrieval arm (web search) exactly once, confirming the research document's own "no loop" framing rather than contradicting it. The pointwise hop toolset, named individually since it is what distinguishes this entry's Graph declaration from every other graph-pass entry's bulk-export one:

- `get_node` — read one node's own record.
- `get_neighbors` — read one node's adjacent edges.
- `get_atomic_facts` — read the atomic facts attached to one node.
- `get_chunk` — read the source chunk backing one node.

### CA-3 — Temporal/episodic memory graphs (graphiti)

Entry path: `decomposed wiring` — graphiti is "internally machine-shaped already: swappable graph drivers (Neo4j, FalkorDB, Kuzu), pluggable LLM clients, embedders, and cross-encoder rerankers," with query-time search needing no LLM at all, so this decomposes cleanly with no opaque core. **Whether the bi-temporal validity intervals need a new `## §14.2` graph-store sub-capability, per this task's own test:** they do not — validity intervals (`valid_at`/`invalid_at`/`created_at`/`expired_at`) are ordinary edge properties any property graph already holds, and the read-side `as_of` semantics is entirely governed by the already-frozen `## §14.4` `temporal` part-declared capability ("bi-temporal validity, contradiction resolution … required before `## §8`'s `as_of` admission condition is satisfied"), which operates at the query boundary rather than requiring the store itself to know about time. This finding bears on, without itself resolving, `PARTS.md ## Appendix A`'s still-open `DR-05` row — that document's own to close, not this one's. No `## §R` repair is forced. [code-verified] [MODALITIES-CODE-AND-ALIEN Part 2 §3] [CONTRACT §14.4]

Node kinds: `extractor` (row 3 — episode ingestion plus LLM entity/edge extraction with temporal bounds, `calls_llm`; nearest-signature for the MinHash-plus-LLM dedupe pass and the deterministic date-arithmetic contradiction-invalidation pass, since both derive new or updated graph content with no query/LLM call of their own, the same nearest-signature convention `GR-2`'s `fact-edges` row already establishes); `retriever` (row 4 — hybrid BM25 plus cosine plus graph-BFS search); `join` — a `## §13.2` D6-repaired fusion policy (RRF/MMR), the same fan-in shape `VT-3`'s own join already exercises; `ranker/reranker` (row 7 — the optional cross-encoder rerank). [code-verified] [MODALITIES-CODE-AND-ALIEN Part 2 §3] [CONTRACT §13.4] [CONTRACT §13.2]

The bi-temporal split, stated because it is the clean split this entry's own extraction node carries forward from the source: the LLM's job ends at extraction (probabilistic — which entities, which edges, what bounds); the invalidation decision is deterministic date arithmetic (`old.invalid_at = new.valid_at`, `old.expired_at = now()`) — no LLM call anywhere in the contradiction-resolution path itself.

This split is exactly the probabilistic-extraction/deterministic-resolution boundary a conformant `extractor` node's own internals should draw, per the nearest-signature convention this field already invokes.

Declared capabilities: `## §14.4`'s `temporal` capability (declared and hosted, satisfying `## §8` condition 11's `as_of` admission gate); ordinary Graph store writes carrying the four timestamp fields as edge properties, needing no new `## §14.2` sub-capability per the finding above; `## §14.2`'s Vector namespaces (OpenAI/Gemini/BGE embeddings); a `join` node declaring an RRF/MMR fusion policy per `## §13.2`'s D6 repair. ### CA-5 — Structured-data RAG (text-to-SQL / table QA)

Entry path: `partial with opaque core` — the schema-slice retrieval half (introspect schema, sample rows, embed schema elements) is an ordinary decomposed wiring writing into the machine's own Vector/KV stores; the SQL-execution half is governed entirely by `CONTRACT.md §15.2`'s already-run worked case — **cited, never re-decided**: "external system-of-record SQL … is not a storage need this ladder decides; it is a node-admission need," redirected to `## §17`'s foreign-part adapter (`external-tool`, row 10, hosted `opaque`). **This entry and `MC-1`/`MC-2` (task 3) apply the identical §15.2/§17 disposition to two different concretes** — a generic text-to-SQL part's own ad hoc external RDBMS connection here, versus a served MCP tool reaching a Postgres/Supabase-administered one there — and neither entry re-decides the ruling both cite. [inference] [CONTRACT §15.2] [CONTRACT §17]

Node kinds: `chunker`/`embedder` (schema elements and column descriptions, ordinary Vector store writes, rows 1/2); `retriever` (row 4, schema-slice retrieval); `rewriter` (row 6 — the LLM SQL-generation call, emitting the query text handed to the opaque node's own manifest-declared input, not a `## §18.1` public `formal_query` member, since this crossing happens inside a wiring rather than at the seam); `external-tool` (row 10, hosted `opaque` per `## §17`, accepting the generated SQL and emitting `sql_result_set` per `## §4`'s own ItemKind union); `assembler`/`generator` (rows 8/9, optional result summarization). [inference] [MODALITIES-CODE-AND-ALIEN Part 2 §5] [CONTRACT §13.4] [CONTRACT §4]

The `rewriter`-to-`external-tool` crossing, named because it is the one boundary a reader could mistake for the seam's own `formal_query` member: it is not — `## §18.1`'s `formal_query` governs a query object arriving *at the seam*, while this crossing sits entirely inside one wiring, between two of its own nodes, and is typed by the opaque node's own manifest-declared accepted input instead.

Declared capabilities: ordinary `## §14.2` Vector namespaces for the schema-slice half; `## §4`'s `sql_result_set` ItemKind crossing the adapter boundary; the opaque execution node's full `## §8` admission burden as restated at `## §17` (whole-closure environment hash, denied network namespace or `unbudgetable` exclusion, wall-clock ceiling, `storage: self-contained`, exclusion from the default selector while opaque). **Query-time execution failure is a structured-error-channel need, not a retrieval-shaped one** — the research document's own framing, stated here as a declared consequence: "retrieval latency/failure semantics differ (SQL errors, timeouts, permission failures need a structured error channel back through `retrieve`)," because evidence here is *computed* by executing generated SQL against a live system, not looked up against a machine-held index; this entry declares that failure channel via `## §9`'s first-class partial-run rule (a halted or errored execution is traced, scored and tier-labeled, never silently dropped), the identical mechanism `## §17`'s health-and-lifecycle clause already requires of any MCP-served opaque node. [paper-claim] [CONTRACT §17] [CONTRACT §8] [CONTRACT §9]

`## §15.1`'s four-branch procedure, recapped rather than re-run, for the execution half specifically:

- Branch 1 (existing store sub-capability) — fails structurally, since a sub-capability extends a store type the machine itself owns, and an external system-of-record is not data the machine owns.
- Branch 2 (opaque blob artifact) — fails, since the data is live and never copied into the machine's own artifact store; there is nothing to content-address.
- Branch 3 (`self_storage`) — fails, since `self_storage` scopes what a producing instance privately writes, and the machine never writes this system-of-record at all.
- Branch 4 (new store kind) — resolves negatively and redirects: no sixth native store type is needed; the need is a node-admission need, decided at `## §8`/`## §17`.

Artifact scopes: `quarantined` for the schema-embedding half — the same majority disposition every freshly-decomposed breadth entry in this catalog carries; **`n-a — external, never machine-held`** for the execution half, per `## §15.2`'s own finding for this exact case: "the data is not static, machine-held bytes; it is a live, independently-administered system … there is nothing to content-address." [inference] [CONTRACT §3] [CONTRACT §15.2]

Port cost class: **recut** — the decomposed schema-retrieval half is the bulk of what is ported, with the opaque execution core layered on top as an admission cost, the same mixed partial-with-opaque-core disposition `GR-3`'s and `GR-6`'s own entries already carry `recut` under. [inference] [CONTEXT D-16]

Buckets (discard/replace/recut/carry over): Discard — none unique beyond the ordinary loop-ownership discard every breadth entry in this catalog already states. **Replace** — none for the execution half, since there is nothing to content-address per the artifact-scopes finding above; the schema-embedding half's store backend rewired to the machine's Vector client. Recut — the schema-introspection→embed→retrieve chain recut into `chunker`/`embedder`/`retriever` primitive nodes. Carry over — the SQL-generation prompt template and the schema-description heuristics, carried as the `rewriter` node's own internals; the whole external-tool execution core carried wholesale as the opaque node's internals, per `## §17`'s "admit a fully foreign, unmodified engine" framing, the identical framing `CA-2`'s own carry-over bucket already states. [inference] [MODALITIES-CODE-AND-ALIEN Part 2 §5] [CONTRACT §17]

Advantages retained at entry: `ADV-1` (rig comparability), `ADV-2` (budget enforcement), `ADV-3` (trace attribution), `ADV-5` (swap-invariance at the seam), `ADV-6` (versioning/promote-rollback) — retained for the decomposed schema-retrieval half on the same grounds any decomposed wiring earns them; the opaque execution node is excluded from the default selector while opaque (`## §8` condition 7) without losing its own place in `## §5`'s gate or `## §0`'s registration. [inference] [CONTRACT §5] [CONTRACT §4] [CONTRACT §18.2] [CONTRACT §0] [CONTRACT §8]

Advantages decomposition would earn: `ADV-4` (artifact sharing) — earned, not yet retained, for the schema-retrieval half's nodes once their effective depth reaches `stage`; the execution half can never earn this advantage at all, since its data is never machine-held per `## §15.2`'s own finding. [inference] [CONTRACT §3] [CONTRACT §15.2]

### CA-6 — Cache-augmented / long-context (CAG)

Entry path: `decomposed wiring`, the two-node floor — "there is no index and no retrieval," per the research document's own framing; ingest reduces to corpus concatenation and answering reduces to one LLM call, exercising the universal-core disjunction directly ("ingest(source) + at least one of retrieve | answer") rather than any retrieval-shaped capability. [paper-claim] [MODALITIES-CODE-AND-ALIEN Part 2 §6]

Node kinds: `assembler` (row 8, `order_policy: "whole corpus, no ranking"` — the entire ingested corpus as one `ContextPackage` block); `generator` (row 9, declaring `## §14.3`'s `prompt_cache` flag and `requires_block_kinds` matching the assembler's single whole-corpus block). No `chunker`, `embedder`, `retriever` or `ranker/reranker` node exists anywhere on this record — the sparsest wiring in this catalog, two primitive-part nodes against `VT-1`'s own six-node floor. [paper-claim] [MODALITIES-CODE-AND-ALIEN Part 2 §6] [CONTRACT §13.4]

Declared capabilities: `## §14.3`'s `prompt_cache` flag — mandatory, its own table naming CAG as the motivating case ("the part depends on prompt-cache control (CAG's whole-corpus cached prompt)"), degradation path "no caching, paying the full prefill cost per run"; a corpus-size gate via `## §9`'s capacity/spend split (declared max corpus tokens). **Explicitly not** `## §14.4`'s `pass-through-retrieve` — that capability presumes a `retrieve()` call still exists, unranked; CAG has no `retrieve()` call at all, a distinction this entry states so the two are not conflated. [paper-claim] [CONTRACT §14.3] [CONTRACT §14.4] [CONTRACT §9]

Artifact scopes: `quarantined` — an ordinary `writes_artifact` write for the durable, indefinitely-reused corpus cache, fresh and not yet at `stage` depth; this is not the `## §2` transient `writes_kv` member, since the cache is reused across many queries rather than scoped to one run. [inference] [CONTRACT §3] [CONTRACT §2]

Port cost class: **rewire** — the reference approach's own "orchestration shape" (concatenate, then answer) survives entirely intact; the only real port cost is rewiring whatever direct-API cache-control mechanism a reference implementation uses to the machine's own `prompt_cache`-flagged role-addressable client — there is no entangled orchestration to recut and nothing of substance to carry over, the identical `rewire` character `VT-1`'s own anchor already establishes, here for the second time on genuinely distinct evidence. [inference] [CONTEXT D-16]

Buckets (discard/replace/recut/carry over): Discard — none; no orchestration exists to discard at all — no loop, no retrieval, no ranking — matching `VT-1`'s own "already sequential" framing but sparser still. **Replace** — the prompt-cache client call, rewired to the machine's own `prompt_cache`-flagged role-addressable client; this is the dominant, and effectively the only substantive, bucket. Recut — none; the two-node wiring is already at primitive granularity, with nothing entangled to recut. **Carry over — none of substance**, stated as an empty-with-reason cell per `## §V`'s "both extremes" framing: no formula, no ontology, and no prompt template thick enough to name as a carried asset exists in a bare concatenate-then-answer shape. **The two-direction clause, stated once so no reader and no later entry blurs the two.** MCP is one protocol serving two contracts, and `MC-1`/`MC-2` below are entries on one side of that split only. The machine as MCP **server** is the public seam (`CONTRACT §18`, `ANATOMY` entry 1 `SEAM`) — a consumer selects a modality without naming a wiring, and `§18.5`'s tool-per-operation surface-growth rule binds: a new tool earns admission only for a genuinely new operation, never for a second modality answering one an existing tool already exposes. The machine as MCP **client** is the foreign-part adapter (`CONTRACT §17`, `ANATOMY` entry 27 `ADAPTER`) — a foreign server is admitted as an `external-tool` node (`§13.4` row 10) hosted `opaque` per `§8`'s eleven admission conditions. Two contracts, one protocol. `MC-1` and `MC-2` are entries on the `§17` adapter side only, and nothing in either entry licenses any claim about the `§18` seam side — a reader looking for evidence about the machine's own served surface finds it at `ANATOMY` entry 1, never here. [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §18] [CONTRACT §17]

### MC-1 — postgres-mcp

Entry path: `external-tool` (`§13.4` row 10) hosted `opaque` (`## §17`) — settled by `## §15.3`'s already-run verdict, cited rather than re-decided: external system-of-record SQL "lands on none of the four storage-need branches — Branch 4's test resolves it as a node-admission need rather than a storage need," redirected to `## §17`'s foreign-part adapter exactly as `## §15.2`'s worked case names `postgres-mcp` as its own exemplifying concrete for this half. [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §15.3] [CONTRACT §17]

Node kinds: `opaque — one node`, per `## §8`/`## §17`'s admission shape — no topology is drawn, matching `CA-2`'s and `## §X`'s own convention for a whole-engine opaque node. [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §8] [CONTRACT §17]

Declared capabilities: derived from the server's own published tool surface (`crystaldba/postgres-mcp`, `README.md` "MCP Server API" table, nine tools). `execute_sql`/`explain_query`'s raw output types `sql_result_set` per `## §4`'s ItemKind union; the four analysis/recommendation tools' output types `derived_finding` instead, since each is computed *from* the underlying data rather than a raw row set, and `## §4`'s `derived_finding` kind exists exactly for this case ("MUST carry `derived_from: [Ref]`"). The nine tools, grouped by which ItemKind they emit [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §4]:

- `sql_result_set` emitters: `execute_sql` (arbitrary SQL, read/write per Access Mode), `explain_query` (EXPLAIN plans, optionally simulating hypothetical indexes)
- `derived_finding` emitters: `get_top_queries` (slowest queries by total execution time), `analyze_workload_indexes` (index recommendations over the whole workload), `analyze_query_indexes` (index recommendations for up to ten named queries), `analyze_db_health` (buffer cache, connection, constraint, index and vacuum health checks)
- schema-introspection tools, typed as ordinary structured evidence rather than either kind above: `list_schemas`, `list_objects`, `get_object_details`

`storage: self-contained` per `## §8` condition 5 and `## §15.3`'s own template. In **Unrestricted Mode** (the server's own documented "Access Mode," full read/write), `execute_sql`'s write path is a query-time-callable overwrite of the part's own backing store — the connected Postgres database — outside the `writes_artifact`/`## §3`/`## §7` shape, the identical shape `## §14.4`'s `mutable-store` capability and the `mutates_store` effect (X1) already govern; in **Restricted Mode** (read-only transactions only), this surface is denied at the server's own boundary, so the capability is conditionally declared rather than unconditionally. **Query-time execution failure is a structured-error-channel need, not a retrieval-shaped one**: `execute_sql`/`explain_query` compute evidence rather than looking it up, so a SQL error, a statement timeout, or a permission failure at the connected database MUST surface back through `retrieve` as a first-class, traced outcome per `## §9`'s partial-run rule — never silently dropped or retried as if it were an ordinary empty result. [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §4] [CONTRACT §8] [CONTRACT §14.4] [CONTRACT §9] [PARTS-03 X1]

Artifact scopes: `self_storage` as the default — nothing crosses into the machine's own artifact registry in Restricted Mode, and `## §17`'s storage-and-scope clause states plainly "an opaque MCP part MAY write `quarantined` and MUST NOT write `shared`" as the ceiling even where a machine-storage diagnostic cache is later added. [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §17] [CONTRACT §3]

[inference] [PARTS §P] [CONTEXT D-16]

Recut — none; the eleven `## §8` admission verdicts are the exercise, not a topology, matching `## §X`'s own D-04 framing restated here for this catalog's own MC entries. Carry over — the whole nine-tool surface, the index-tuning search algorithm, and the underlying Postgres engine itself, carried over wholesale as the opaque node's internals, per `## §17`'s "admit a fully foreign, unmodified engine" framing — the identical structural disposition `codebase-memory-mcp`'s own `## §P` row already states for its own carry-over cell. **Resolving the YES\* condition against the widened `§13.3` schema, per this task's own instruction.** The sweep's own shape names a "multi-list fusion ranker `List[List[ScoredNode]] -> List[ScoredNode]` keyed on rank, not score" — exactly `§13.3`'s first frozen join shape, fed by arms whose emitted score semantics may differ (each reformulation's own retriever run). `§13.2` ¶4's D6 repair supplies the missing vocabulary directly: a `join` fed by differing-semantics arms MUST declare a fusion policy, and **reciprocal-rank fusion is one of the three named options** ("reciprocal-rank fusion, a declared weighted combination, or round-robin-by-position") — the exact rank-keyed-not-score-keyed fusion this type names. This is the **declared weighted/RRF option**, not LightRAG's own `rank_position` positional-interleave option (`## §L.2`): the two are siblings in D6's three-way enumeration, not the same declaration. ### GS-N5 — Plan-then-retrieve (Plan-RAG / Plan*RAG / TreeRare)

**The sharpest capability gap the sweep found, resolved rather than deferred.** GAP-SWEEP's own CAP-table entry names this the sweep's own sharpest gap: an LLM emits a fresh sub-query DAG or tree *per query*, and the sweep asks whether the contract can express a runtime-planned graph at all, distinct from the versioned, author-declared wiring `§1` freezes. The contract as repaired through Phase 3 already carries the answer in full: `§13.4` row 11 defines `planner` — "query object → an ephemeral-subgraph plan (JSON, recorded verbatim as trace data, never registered/aliased/ledgered per `§11`)" — as one of the fourteen primitive part types, and `§11` states the full lifecycle split GAP-SWEEP's own stress test (`§4.1`) demands: the execution plane MAY invoke the validator on a planner-emitted plan checked against the load-frozen snapshot; the plan MUST be JSON over the declared `NodeKind` alphabet, validated against a restricted allowlist plus the planner's manifest-declared caps, and recorded verbatim as trace data; it MUST NOT be registered, aliased, or ledgered. `§1`'s runtime-minted-instance identity formula — `H(component_instance_hash, JCS(runtime_config), parent_instance_hash, ordinal)` — gives every node the planner emits a computable identity with no author-supplied `config_hash` to hash. **This entry states plainly: a runtime-planned DAG is expressible under the contract as repaired through Phase 3, with no amendment required.** `GS-N21`'s DRIFT entry (`04-04`) already exercises exactly this mechanism (a `planner` spawning local follow-up queries from a global seed), so this is a proven, not merely a paper, pathway. [inference] [CONTRACT §13.4] [CONTRACT §11] [CONTRACT §1] [CATALOG GS-N21]

Entry path: `decomposed wiring` — a `planner` node reads a query object and emits an ephemeral-subgraph plan; the executor invokes the validator on that plan against the load-frozen snapshot, then executes it as ordinary `fanout`/`retriever`/`join` nodes; no new rung. [inference] [CONTRACT §13.4] [CONTRACT §11]

[inference] [CONTRACT §13.4] [CONTRACT §2] [CONTRACT §13.3]

**Resolving the structural gap this type forces, rather than passing through the sweep's own verdict.** GAP-SWEEP's own shape ("`map(pipeline, N) → reduce(vote)` at *whole-pipeline* level") requires an N-way fan-in over N complete generations' worth of candidate answers. Before this plan's own repair, `§13.4` row 9 stated flatly that `Answer` "is outside the `ItemKind` evidence union... not evidence flowing across a `deps` edge," with no exception for a non-`provides`-named generator — under that unamended text, no join or grader could legally consume a candidate answer at all, and this type's own "reduce(vote)" step was **not expressible**, contrary to the sweep's own YES\* verdict taken at face value. This is exactly the `D-07` case this task's own prohibition names: the type is not recorded as expressible on the sweep's verdict alone. The gap is repaired in place at `§13.1`/`§13.4` row 9 (`## §R` row `G1`, jointly forced with `GS-N25` and `GS-N28`), minting `Draft`'s schema and distinguishing a `provides`-named generator's genuine terminal `Answer` from a `drafter`/`verifier`-bound generator's `Draft`, which does cross a `deps` edge. [inference] [PARTS-06 GS-N24] [CONTRACT §13.1] [CONTRACT §13.4]

**Budget ownership, the task's own named distinctive question, answered rather than left open.** The `fanout` node's own multiplicative accounting (`§9`: "an `N`-way fan-out's total spend is the sum of `N` independently-budgeted branches") already prices the N whole-pipeline copies, subgraph promotion notwithstanding — a `subgraph` node is still a node, subject to the same fan-out rule as any primitive part. The join/vote step's own budget is a **declared apportionment at the join node** itself, per `§9`'s D4 merge-side-apportionment repair, exactly as any other join's budget is closed on the merge side. No new budget object (a "convergence/consensus halting" shape, which GAP-SWEEP's own `§3.7` names for the *adaptive*, sample-until-agreement case) is needed here, because this type's own shape is a **fixed-arity** map/reduce (`map(pipeline, N)`, N declared statically), not an adaptive halt — the adaptive case belongs to `N27`'s multi-agent debate, out of this plan's scope. ## §C — Porting protocol

**Satisfies:** PARTS-07

`## §C` is the repeatable method a porter applies to a foreign RAG or LLM-DB system nobody has classified yet: dissection (`### §C.1`), the four-bucket carry-over map (`### §C.2`), the target code shape whatever gets recut must take (`### §C.3`), and the exit test that decides when the port is done (`### §C.4`). The three deep modalities (`GR-1` LightRAG, `GR-2` HippoRAG 2, `CA-2` codebase-memory-mcp) and the sixty-plus breadth entries in `## §E` are cited throughout as worked instances of a step — never as the statement of the step, per the owner's own framing that the three deep wirings demonstrate the method rather than constitute it. [inference] [CONTEXT D-13] [PARTS-07]

### §C.1 — Dissection procedure

A porter dissecting a system nobody has classified yet follows five steps, in order, each producing a recorded artifact a second reader can check, never a decision the first porter reached silently. [inference] [CONTEXT D-13]

1. **Locate the three parts.** Split the foreign system into (a) its **index recipe** — components run once per corpus version, feeding `## §1`'s `recipe` member, producing artifacts many later queries reuse; the observable test is whether a stage's output is keyed to the corpus/recipe version rather than to one query — (b) its **query wiring** — components run once per query, consuming the query object (`## §18.1`) and the recipe's artifacts, eventually contributing to or emitting the terminal `Answer`; the observable test is whether a stage's inputs include the live query and its output feeds toward `## §13.4` row 9's `provides`-named node — and (c) the foreign system's own **machine-owned machinery**: anything duplicating a job this contract already assigns to the machine as executor regardless of who implements it — loop and halt ownership (`## §2`), budget accounting and fan-out multiplication (`## §9`), scheduling/concurrency, artifact-registry and GC mechanics (`## §7`), the promotion ledger (`## §6`); the observable test is not "does the foreign system have its own version of this" but "does the contract already assign this job to the machine," and a positive answer routes the stage to (c) regardless of how elaborate the foreign implementation is. **Intra-node concurrency is not scheduling/concurrency in this sense (§D-1).** A node's own internal fan-out over calls that all exercise one already-declared effect — for example, N concurrent `reads_graph` calls resolving N already-selected candidates — stays inside that node as ordinary internal concurrency and is never itself routed to (c): the "scheduling/concurrency" bullet above names cross-node, cross-stage orchestration only, a scheduler deciding what runs before what *across* nodes, never a node's own concurrent execution of its own already-declared reads. This repair unblocks the dissection step's own test; it does not resolve `## §R N11`'s separate, still-open question of whether `## §9`'s budget shape needs its own declared home for intra-node concurrency, which remains open. [PARTS-07 §D-1] [CONTEXT D-13] [PARTS §R N11] **A stage that appears to sit on both sides** — an index-time step reading query-time state, or a query-time step coupled to an ingest-only structure — is resolved by `## §19.1`'s port-completeness diagnostic directly: lift the crossing value into a declared `deps` edge, or record the two as one fused node with a register row naming the coupling; a stage is never left ambiguously split on the porter's own say-so. LightRAG's `kg_chunk_pick_method` dispatch is the worked illustration: the chunk-selection call fires on every query-time path with only its downstream algorithm varying by config — `## §19.4`'s exclusion clause already rules this config internal to the query-side node, not a boundary straddling the index side. [inference] [CONTRACT §1] [CONTRACT §2] [CONTRACT §9] [CONTRACT §7] [CONTRACT §6] [CONTRACT §18.1] [CONTRACT §19.1] [CONTRACT §19.4]

2. **Enumerate candidate boundaries.** Before cutting anything, apply `## §19.10`'s boundary-enumeration obligation to the foreign system's own code exactly as it already applies to a native part: enumerate every point where the system's effective `effects[]` set would change if cut there, every point where a declared knob (a genuine substitution or bypass point, per `## §19.4`) sits, and every point where a value crosses between two operations. Applied to a foreign system rather than an author's own sketch, the same three minimum enumeration points read against the system's own module and function boundaries — call sites, class boundaries, dispatch branches — rather than against a wiring draft that does not yet exist. The candidate set MUST be recorded alongside the node table it produces, exactly as `## §19.10` already requires, so a second reader can check which boundaries were proposed, not only which of them were cut — the same discipline `PARTS.md ## §L.1`'s and `## §H`'s own node tables already exercised for the two decomposed stress modalities, cited here as worked instances of this step. [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §19.10] [PARTS §L] [PARTS §H]

3. **Apply `## §19`'s criterion at each candidate boundary.** At each candidate boundary from step 2, run `## §19.1` (port completeness — lift or fuse-and-register an unexpressible crossing), `## §19.4` (variance bearing — cut only at a genuine knob or an unavoidable effects-membership forcing, never at a swappable inner algorithm sharing one socket and one effects union), `## §19.5` (attribution closure — a fan-in merging same-item-emitting nodes emits set-valued, machine-stamped provenance), `## §19.6` (opaque-node granularity, for whatever part of the system stays uncut), and `## §19.9` (effects under a selectable strategy — the union over every reachable strategy, including one reached only by a runtime fallback). Record the candidate set together with the verdict reached at each boundary — cut or fuse — so the resulting node set is auditable against step 2's own enumeration, not merely presented as a conclusion. [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §19.1] [CONTRACT §19.4] [CONTRACT §19.5] [CONTRACT §19.6] [CONTRACT §19.9]

4. **Decide the entry rung.** State which of `## Conventions`'s four `Entry path` values the port lands at — `decomposed wiring`, `partial with opaque core`, `whole-engine opaque node`, or `external-tool` — or `out of scope — CONTRACT §N` per `## §V`'s out-of-scope test against `## §12`'s three declared non-goals. Entering at a higher rung than step 3's own cuts would in principle allow is a legitimate stopping point under `## §6`'s incremental, in-place decomposition ratchet, never an abandoned or partial port: `CA-2` (codebase-memory-mcp) enters entirely at `whole-engine opaque node` with nothing decomposed at all, and `MC-1`/`MC-2` enter entirely at `external-tool` under `## §17`'s MCP-specific admission — each is a complete port at its chosen rung, not a partial port of a deeper cut left unfinished. Record which of `## §V`'s six `ADV-1`..`ADV-6` advantages the chosen rung retains and which a deeper cut would additionally earn, in exactly the two-field form every `## §E` entry already carries. [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §6] [CONTRACT §12] [CONTRACT §17] [CONTEXT D-11]

5. **Record the port.** The porter's record is the node set with its candidate boundaries (steps 2–3), the four-bucket assignment (`### §C.2`), the entry rung and its two advantage fields (step 4), the declared capabilities and effects the resulting node set exercises (`## §14`), and the exit-test result (`### §C.4`) — the same nine-field shape `## Conventions` already fixes for a `## §E` entry, so recording a port and drafting its catalog entry are the same act, not two separate ones. [inference] [CONTRACT §14]

**Re-run semantics.** Applying `### §C.1` a second time to an already-ported system yields the same node set and the same bucket assignment for every component that has not itself changed — the dissection is a function of the foreign system's own code and this contract's own criterion, and the passage of time alone alters neither. The one condition that changes a bucket is an upstream release of the foreign system that changes the component itself, not a new reading of an unchanged one. Where that happens, re-run the dissection only over the changed components, and re-run `### §C.4`'s exit test, because the prior parity claim was made against the version that has now changed underneath it. [inference] [CONTEXT D-13]

**Interruption.** A port halted after `### §C.2`'s bucket assignment but before `### §C.4`'s exit test carries no parity claim at all — none is implied by reaching bucket assignment, and none may be recorded. Its partial artifacts are labelled with the step reached (e.g. "bucket assignment complete, exit test not run"), and the port is not entered into the catalog as a completed port — `## Appendix A`'s `pending` status form, not `entry drafted`, names exactly this state — until `### §C.4` actually runs. [inference] [CONTEXT D-13]

### §C.2 — The four-bucket carry-over map

Quoted from `REQUIREMENTS.md`'s own `PARTS-07` wording, the same quotation `## §V` carries after this plan's own correction (see `## §V`'s note): *discard* — "machine already owns it"; *replace* — "rewire to machine clients and stores"; *recut* — "orchestration into primitive-part nodes"; *carry over* — "prompts, ontologies, algorithmic cores as component internals." [docs-verified] [PARTS-07]

**Discard — admission test.** Is this a capability the contract already assigns to the machine as executor — loop/halt ownership, budget accounting, scheduling/concurrency, GC/registry mechanics, promotion-ledger mechanics — regardless of whether the foreign system built its own version of it? This is what separates discard from replace: replace is for a component the machine does **not** already own a version of and merely needs an equivalent client or store for; discard is for a job the machine's own executor already performs, making the foreign implementation redundant rather than merely swappable. [inference] [CONTRACT §2] [CONTRACT §9] [CONTRACT §7] [CONTRACT §6]

**Discard — porter action.** Remove the component's own implementation entirely; do not port its logic and do not carry its configuration forward as a node's internals — name, in the port record, the specific machine mechanism (a `CONTRACT §N` clause) that already performs the discarded function, so a reader can check the discard against a cited replacement rather than an assertion. [inference] [CONTRACT §2]

**Discard — failure mode.** "Reimplementing the executor" — porting a scheduling, looping, or budget-tracking harness as if it were a node's own internal logic, silently duplicating what `## §2`/`## §9` already own and producing a wiring that fights the machine's own loop ownership at run time. [inference] [CONTRACT §2] [CONTRACT §9]

**Discard — worked citation.** `GR-1`'s own discard bucket: LightRAG's async priority-scheduling/gathering harness (`_priority`, the per-call `asyncio.gather` orchestration), discarded outright because `## §2`'s executor-owns-every-loop rule and `## §9`'s budget shape make loop and halt ownership the machine's job. [code-verified] [PARTS §P]

**Replace — admission test.** Does this component call an external service or hold a store backend the machine has its own equivalent client or store type for (`## §14.2`/`## §14.3`), where the component's own surrounding orchestration still has a job to do once that call is rewired? This separates replace from recut: replace touches only a component's client/store binding at its edges; recut is required once the component's own **internal** orchestration — not merely its store or client calls — needs decomposing into primitive-part nodes. [inference] [CONTRACT §14.2] [CONTRACT §14.3]

**Replace — porter action.** Rewrite the concrete backend or client call to the machine's injected client or store, declare the corresponding `## §14.2`/`## §14.3` capability the new binding actually exercises, and leave the surrounding logic as the resulting node's internals if the component becomes one node — or continue into the recut bucket if that surrounding logic itself must decompose. [inference] [CONTRACT §14.2] [CONTRACT §14.3]

**Replace — failure mode.** "The raw client masquerading as a port" — swapping a backend URL or library for the machine's own client without declaring the resulting `## §14.3` capability flags (`logprobs`, `prefix_continuation`, `prompt_cache`, `requires_model`) or checking `## §14.3`'s role-versus-priority-class separation, producing a component that silently exercises an undeclared capability the validator never had a chance to check. [inference] [CONTRACT §14.3]

**Replace — worked citation.** `VT-1`'s replace bucket: the ANN index backend (HNSW/IVF) and the optional cross-encoder reranker API, each rewired to the machine's Vector client (`## §14.2`) and role-addressable rerank client (`## §14.3`) — the port-cost anchor for the `rewire` class on `## §V`'s scale. [code-verified] [PARTS §V]

**Recut — admission test.** Does the component's own orchestration route data between what would become two or more `## §13.4` primitive-part nodes — does it host more than one distinguishable operation, per `## §19`'s variance-bearing and boundary-enumeration criteria applied at step 3 of `### §C.1`? This separates recut from carry over: recut is the orchestration shape itself — the sequencing and dataflow between operations; carry over is what runs **inside** a resulting node once the orchestration around it is gone. [inference] [CONTRACT §19.4] [CONTRACT §19.10]

**Recut — porter action.** Apply `### §C.1`'s steps 2–3 (candidate-boundary enumeration under `## §19.10`, cut decisions under `## §19.1`–`## §19.9`) to the component's internals, and record the resulting node positions with their `## §13.4` types. [inference] [CONTRACT §19.10]

**Recut — failure mode.** "The transcription trap" — recording a recut as a retelling of how one of the three deep modalities was itself recut, rather than deriving the cut fresh from `## §19`'s criterion applied to the new component's own boundaries; this is the exact failure this plan's own prohibition names for `## §C` as a whole, restated here at the bucket where it is most tempting. [inference] [CONTEXT D-13]

**Recut — worked citation.** `GR-1`'s seventeen of eighteen query-side node positions recut over an opaque, carried-over ingest core; `GS-N24`'s promotion of a base wiring to a `subgraph`+`fanout`(N)+`join`(vote) chain — a genuine orchestration addition this plan's own `G1` repair was required to make expressible at all. [code-verified] [PARTS §L] [PARTS-06 G1]

**Carry over — admission test.** Once the three tests above are exhausted, does what remains inside a node's own boundary — a prompt template, an ontology, an algorithmic formula, a scoring heuristic — have no separately varying, separately measurable interface of its own, per `## §19.1`'s port-completeness diagnostic, so that it is configuration or internals rather than a cut boundary? This separates carry over from recut: an internal that **does** expose a knob per `## §19.4`(a) (a genuine substitution or bypass point) belongs in recut as its own node, not folded silently into carry over. [inference] [CONTRACT §19.1] [CONTRACT §19.4]

**Carry over — porter action.** Leave the item as the resulting node's internal logic with no further decomposition and no re-derivation, but name it specifically in the port record — the prompt text, the formula, the model call — rather than a vague "the rest carries over." [inference] [CONTRACT §19.1]

**Carry over — failure mode.** "Silent algorithm laundering" — carrying an algorithmic core forward without recording what it is, so a later reader cannot tell "deliberately not decomposed, evidence attached" from "not looked at." [inference] [CONTEXT D-16]

**Carry over — worked citation.** `GR-2`'s carry-over bucket: the OpenIE extraction prompts and the PPR algorithm run via `igraph`'s native `prpack` implementation rather than reimplemented, carried inside `ppr-retriever`'s own internals; `GR-1`'s prompt templates and round-robin interleave/dedup logic, carried inside its own join nodes. [code-verified] [PARTS §P]

**Both extremes, stated before any real case is judged against them**, the same shape `PARTS.md ## §P` already uses: a system whose components carry over wholly reads as three empty buckets (discard, replace, recut), each with a stated reason, and one full carry-over bucket, anchoring `## §V`'s cheapest `rehost` class — `CA-2` (codebase-memory-mcp) is this record's own instance, three of its four buckets empty by construction because nothing is decomposed at all. A system that must be wholly rewritten reads as an empty carry-over bucket with a stated reason, anchoring the costliest `rebuild` class — `GS-N32` (FiD) is this record's own instance, its carry-over bucket empty because the multi-passage decode mode it needs "no LLM API exposes," per its own entry, leaving nothing of the incumbent's own approach with a home in any other bucket either. [docs-verified] [PARTS §P] [PARTS §X] [PARTS-06 GS-N32]

**The rule this map inherits, unamended.** Where two systems land in the same summary class, they remain two entries with their own evidence — the class is a summary of the buckets and is never a merge key, `PARTS.md ## §P`'s own rule, already exercised on this record: `GR-1` and `GR-2` both map to `recut`, and stand as two rows, not one. [docs-verified] [PARTS §P]

**The fork case (D-13, §D-2).** [inference] Nothing above states what a porter does when a candidate boundary's own code is byte-identical, or differs only in a mechanically negligible way (variable names, formatting, no behavioural delta), to a component already dissected and bucketed in an existing `## §E` entry — `### §C.1`'s own "Re-run semantics" paragraph covers only the *same* system's dissection re-run over time, never a *different* system sharing code with an already-ported one. **Repair:** the porter MAY cite that existing entry's bucket assignment for the identical component in place of re-deriving it, provided the citation names the existing entry and the specific component, and the porter has actually verified byte-identity or negligible-diff status rather than assumed it from family resemblance — a component that has diverged even slightly is dissected fresh, in full, per `### §C.1`'s ordinary procedure. **The port-cost class question, answered directly:** a fork's port-cost class is never inherited from a cited port's class; it is computed exactly as for any other system, from the fork's own four-bucket dominance over its own component set — a cited-and-verified component still occupies whichever one of the four buckets it was originally found in, it does not create a fifth "inherited" category, and the class calculation counts it there. [PARTS-07 §D-2] [CONTEXT D-13]

### §C.3 — Target code shape

This is the one part of the protocol PARTS-07 requires to be code-verified, so every clause below carries the tag its evidence actually supports and no clause is tagged above its evidence — a claim about code observed is `[code-verified]`; a claim about the not-yet-written recut form is `[inference]`. [inference] [PARTS-07]

1. **Injected primitives.** A recut component receives its LLM client, embed client, rerank client and store handles as injected dependencies carrying declared capability requirements (`## §14.3`'s role and flag table), and constructs none of them itself; `## §2`'s undeclared-is-denied rule bounds what a node may reach at all. LightRAG's `kg_query` (`operate.py:4398`) already indirects its query-role call through `use_model_func = partial(global_config["role_llm_funcs"]["query"], _priority=DEFAULT_QUERY_PRIORITY)` (`operate.py:4445`) — a callable retrieved by string key from an untyped `global_config: dict[str, str]` parameter, with no declared client flag anywhere in the signature (no `logprobs`/`prefix_continuation`/`prompt_cache`/`requires_model`) and no role-separation guarantee beyond the dict-key convention `## §14.3`'s own D12 repair already found insufficient (LightRAG's `bypass` path requests a different priority class for the same `query` role, which a bare dict key cannot express). A porter must not mistake this indirection for conformant injection: a callable reached via a config dict is not a declared, capability-checked client. What changes on recut: the node's manifest declares its role binding and any client flags it depends on per `## §14.3`'s table, and the machine resolves the actual client at wire time — the node's own code never looks one up in a dict. [code-verified] [inference] [CONTRACT §14.3] [CONTRACT §2]

2. **Plain-data boundaries.** A recut component's inputs and outputs are typed plain data compared across a `deps` edge under `## §13.2`, matching one of `## §13.4`'s entry-point signatures. `kg_query`'s own declared return type, `QueryResult | None` (`base.py:1693`), is a dataclass carrying `content: Optional[str]`, `response_iterator: Optional[AsyncIterator[str]]`, and `raw_data: Optional[Dict[str, Any]]` — the exemplar function's output maps loosely toward `## §13.4` row 9's `generator`/`Answer` family, but `response_iterator` is a **live async-generator handle**, not a resolvable reference, and `raw_data: Dict[str, Any]` is untyped, defeating `## §13.2`'s explicit-discriminator requirement. A non-conforming boundary is named directly here rather than left abstract: a boundary that passes a live handle (the `AsyncIterator`), a closure (`use_model_func` itself, passed onward rather than resolved locally), or an engine-specific untyped blob (`Dict[str, Any]`) is exactly what this property forbids. What changes on recut: the node's output socket carries an `## §13.4` row 9 typed `Answer` — or `Draft`, per the `G1` repair, where the node is not `provides`-named — never a live iterator handle or an unstructured dict. **A fourth violation form, found by this plan's own dry-run (§D-3):** a shared mutable argument object written by one candidate operation and read by a later one as an implicit signal is exactly the same category of forbidden crossing as a live handle, since neither travels as plain typed data across a declared socket; on recut, the signal becomes an explicit typed field on the `deps` edge between the two resulting nodes, or, where the two operations fuse into one node under `## §19.4`, the signal simply disappears as an internal local variable, no longer crossing anything. [code-verified] [inference] [CONTRACT §13.2] [CONTRACT §13.4] [PARTS-06 G1] [PARTS-07 §D-3]

3. **Declared effects.** Every store read or write, LLM call, embedding call and rerank call a recut component can reach is declared in `effects[]` under `## §2`'s undeclared-is-denied rule, over the union of reachable strategies per `## §19.9` — including a strategy reached only through a runtime fallback path. A porter derives the effect set by walking every call site the `### §C.1` step-2 enumeration already surfaced and classifying each against `## §2`'s sixteen-member vocabulary. LightRAG's response-cache read/write inside the query path (`handle_cache`, `utils.py:3834-3841`, flushed at `operate.py:4863`) is the case this contract's own `D1` repair was forced by: it is a call site inside the query wiring a porter enumerating `kg_query`'s calls could easily miss, and missing it under-declares the node's effects exactly as it under-declared `## §2`'s original vocabulary before `writes_kv`/`reads_kv` existed to name it. The trap is named plainly: an effect reached only through a fallback is still reachable, and `## §19.9`'s own ruling states this is not an exempt corner case. [code-verified] [inference] [CONTRACT §2] [CONTRACT §19.9] [PARTS-04 D1]

4. **No direct store handles.** A recut component never holds a backend driver, connection, or file handle; all store access goes through the machine's store clients under `## §14.2`'s sub-capabilities. `kg_query`'s own signature (`operate.py:4398-4408`) is the concrete, named form of the violation this property forbids: `knowledge_graph_inst: BaseGraphStorage`, `entities_vdb: BaseVectorStorage`, `relationships_vdb: BaseVectorStorage`, `text_chunks_db: BaseKVStorage`, `chunks_vdb: BaseVectorStorage`, `hashing_kv: BaseKVStorage | None` — six live storage-backend instances passed directly as function parameters, not resolved references and not machine-mediated reads. What changes on recut: the node declares `reads_graph`/`reads_vector`/`reads_kv` (as applicable) in `effects[]`, and the machine resolves the actual store instance at load per `## §14.2`'s `read-namespace` sub-capability (the `D2` repair) — the node's own code never receives a `BaseGraphStorage` object, only whatever typed evidence its declared reads produce. [code-verified] [inference] [CONTRACT §14.2] [PARTS-04 D2]

**The `operate.py` verification — Evidence:** file `lightrag/operate.py`, functions `kg_query` (signature and dispatch, `operate.py:4398-4456`; `use_model_func` construction at `:4445`, mirrored at `:4832` and in `naive_query`'s own construction at `:6412`) and `naive_query` (`operate.py:6352-6377`); `QueryResult` (`lightrag/base.py:1693-1721`). Clone repository `HKUDS/LightRAG`, commit `b93f7c31f10880bcaccc9518c6a7582fbbe94f1b` — an existing clone found under a prior session's scratchpad rather than freshly re-cloned for this task; its `operate.py`/`lightrag.py` line counts (6,631/6,830) match `PARTS.md ## §P`'s own cited "pinned research clone" counts exactly, so every citation above is checkable against the same clone `PARTS.md`'s own LightRAG decomposition was drawn from, not a different one presented as if it were that clone. Every claim above about what the code does is `[code-verified]` and cites function and line; every claim about what the recut form would look like is `[inference]` and is stated as such — the recut form does not exist yet. [code-verified] [inference] [PARTS §P]

**The Databasise fork cross-check (D-15).** The archive at `/home/chris/Vibe Coding/Databasise/_archives/sourcerer-lightrag_nonstripped_2026-06-26.tgz` was already extracted read-only under this phase's earlier work (`/tmp/databasise-check/sourcerer-lightrag/lightrag`, not re-extracted or modified here). This cross-check finds the **same disagreement** `PARTS.md ## §P`'s own cross-check paragraph already recorded (agrees on architecture, disagrees on version — the fork pins `1.5.4` against the pinned research clone's later `1.5.7`, confirmed here rather than left unnamed), so that paragraph is cited rather than restated. **Closing scope line.** `### §C.3` states the shape a recoded part must take and does not prescribe a language, a framework, a directory layout, or a test harness. No code spike is written in this phase; the design-documents fence holds. [inference] [CONTEXT D-13]

### §C.4 — Exit test

**The parity statement.** A port exits when the ported system and the original, run under the same `bundle@v` and the same tier, produce a delta inside the per-`(bundle@v, tier, metric)` A/A band — each term a named interface the porter is **given**, never designed: `bundle@v` is a frozen eval-bundle instance per `## §10` (questions, gold answers, a judge instance, a corpus snapshot hash); tier is one of `## §4`'s `T0`–`T3` evidence tiers, or `## §5`'s "greatest common depth" where the two arms' depths differ; the A/A band is the null `## §5` calibrates per `(bundle@v, tier, metric)` and sets its promotion floor at, at that null's p95; the delta is `## §5`'s own paired statistic (exact McNemar for a binary outcome, paired bootstrap for a continuous one) run at that depth. [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §5] [CONTRACT §4] [CONTRACT §10]

**The baseline arm.** The original, unported system runs as an `external-tool` node (`## §13.4` row 10) admitted `opaque` under `## §8`'s eleven conditions — the same admission path `CA-2`, `MC-1` and `MC-2` already use: a validated manifest, a whole-closure environment hash, a denied network namespace with the machine as injected LLM provider (or `unbudgetable` exclusion from token comparisons, per `## §4`'s explicit-refusal rule), a declared wall-clock ceiling, a declared `storage: machine | self-contained`, a declared `feed_tier`, exclusion from the default selector while `opaque`, and a 90-day renewable TTL. Where the original is admitted `unbudgetable`, any token-count comparison between the two arms is refused per `## §4`'s rule — the parity comparison proceeds on non-token metrics, and a token-based metric requires the baseline accept the machine's injected provider. Whether the baseline arm stays admissible alongside the ported arm where the two arms' node sets could coincide under some runtime data is governed by `## §19.8`'s equal-node-set rule; `03-D8-DISPOSITION-AUDIT.md` found that none of that rule's three permitted outcomes — a declared-guard variant, collision as one identity, or distinct arms by another declared field — **expels** an arm from a comparison, so this exit test does not state, and the contract does not carry, a categorical rule that would refuse a baseline-vs-port comparison merely because the arms could converge; whichever of the three outcomes applies, the baseline stays comparable under one of them. [docs-verified] [inference] [CONTRACT §8] [CONTRACT §13.4] [CONTRACT §4] [CONTRACT §19.8] [03-D8-DISPOSITION-AUDIT]

**What parity is not.** Parity is not gain: `## §5`'s own "parity, not gain" clause states a decomposition is gated on parity, its measured difference from the pre-decomposition original falling inside the A/A band or naming its declared deviations explicitly — a port that scores higher than the original has not passed a stronger test, and an unexplained improvement after a supposedly behaviour-preserving port is evidence of a measurement error or an undeclared behaviour change, not a bonus. No benchmark number settles any part of this exit test, per the project's own evidence standard, restated here because a porting protocol is exactly where the overclaim this clause forbids is most tempting to make. [docs-verified] [CONTRACT §5]

**What a failed exit test means.** A failed exit test has a defined next move, never an open end: re-enter `### §C.1` at the step the failure implicates — step 3's cut decisions if the delta traces to a mis-cut boundary, or step 1's part-location if the failure traces to a coupling that was never lifted — or record the port as entering at a higher rung under `## §6`'s decomposition ratchet, keeping the advantages that rung retains and naming which the failed deeper cut would have earned instead. `## §6`'s own framing governs here exactly as it governs `## §8`'s admission conditions: a restriction not yet earned, never a permanent prohibition. [inference] [CONTRACT §6]

**The scope fence, in `PARTS.md ## §P`'s own wording — named here, not designed here.** The A/A calibration procedure per `(bundle@v, tier, metric)` is `RIG-06`'s, closed in `RIG.md ## §AA`; eval-bundle versioning, sizing from the target MDE, and the settled affordability verdict are `RIG-03`'s and `RIG-04`'s, closed in `RIG.md ## §EV` and `## §F3`; the baseline-arm's own run mechanics — the parallel-run model, shared KV, per-part namespaces, fan-out query, side-by-side output comparison — are `RIG-01`'s, closed in `RIG.md ## §RUN`; the trace schema instance hashes, depth, tier and budget-state fields this exit test reads are `RIG-02`'s, closed in `RIG.md ## §TR`; the promote/rollback protocol end to end is `RIG-05`'s, closed in `RIG.md ## §PR`; and the run lifecycle — how a run halts, how a partial run is scored and labelled, whether it resumes — is `RIG-07`'s, closed in `RIG.md ## §LC`. ROADMAP Phase 5 owns `RIG-01` through `RIG-07` in full; this clause names them and stops. [docs-verified] [CONTEXT D-14] [PARTS §P] [RIG §RUN] [RIG §TR] [RIG §EV] [RIG §F3] [RIG §PR] [RIG §AA] [RIG §LC]

Every step below states which `### §C.n` clause it applied and whether that clause was sufficient; every insufficiency found is registered as a numbered finding, repaired in place in `## §C`, and given a `## §R` row.
