**1e. The node set (`### §C.1` step 5).** Six query-side positions: `rewriter` (keyword extraction, `operate.py:493-494`), `retriever` (`entity-lookup`, the local/ll arm's vector+graph fetch inside `_get_node_data`, nearest-signature basis matching `## §H`'s own `fact-score`/`ppr` convention for a fused lookup-and-hydrate shape), `retriever` (`path-enumerator`, the hl arm's fused path-enumeration-and-scoring node inside `_find_most_related_edges_from_entities3`, nearest-signature basis: its operative read is the Graph store's bulk-export sub-capability, exactly `## §H`'s own `ppr`-retriever precedent for a bulk-export-based retriever with no pointwise query of its own), `join` (`combine_contexts`, `operate.py:995-1009`), `assembler` (the context-template rendering in `_build_query_context`'s return, `operate.py:663-676`), `generator` (the answer-generation call in `kg_query`, `operate.py:551-561`). No `embedder` node is authored separately — the query-time embedding call is folded inside `entities_vdb.query()`/`relationships_vdb.query()` (`operate.py:695,910` — store-side, not a cut boundary PathRAG's own code exposes any differently than `## §H`'s `chunk-embed` convention already treats a fused store-call embedding). [code-verified]

### Step 2 — Bucket assignment (`### §C.2`)

Applying the four admission tests to the node set from Step 1, deriving every bucket fresh from PathRAG's own code rather than citing `GR-1`'s bucket assignment for the shared components — this is where D-13 predicted the fork case would bite, and it does, in a stronger form than a simple "cite the parent" shortcut would have caught:

**Discard.** The synchronous-wrapper shim (`always_get_an_event_loop`/`run_until_complete`, `PathRAG.py:80-105`) — machine-owned per Step 1's loop-ownership test, `## §2`. **This is PathRAG's entire discard bucket.** A porter who assumed a fork's discard bucket simply restates its parent's would have been wrong here: `GR-1`'s discard bucket is LightRAG's whole `_priority`/`asyncio.gather` priority-scheduling harness (`### §C.2`'s own worked citation) — code PathRAG's fork never carried forward at all (confirmed by grep: PathRAG's `operate.py` has no `_priority` construct anywhere). PathRAG forked LightRAG's *dispatch and topology*, not its *scheduler* — a fork can diverge from its parent's bucket population even while sharing most of its query-wiring shape, and only a fresh read of the fork's own code catches this; a cited bucket would have silently imported a scheduling harness that was never there to discard. [code-verified]

**Replace.** The three concrete storage backends (`JsonKVStorage`/`NanoVectorDBStorage`/`NetworkXStorage`, set via the `kv_storage`/`vector_storage`/`graph_storage` dataclass fields, `PathRAG.py:118-120`) and the raw client functions (`openai_complete`/`openai_embedding` defaults for `llm_model_func`/`embedding_func`, `PathRAG.py:150,154`) — rewired to the machine's own store/LLM/embed clients. Independently re-derived from PathRAG's own `__post_init__` field list; the shape matches `GR-1`'s own replace bucket (three store backends plus a raw client wrapper) because both systems genuinely share this code, not because one was cited from the other. [code-verified]

**Recut.** The six query-side node positions from Step 1e. The `path-enumerator` node is the fork's one genuinely new orchestration LightRAG's own port never needed — LightRAG's `global`-arm relationship retrieval is a direct top-k lookup (`## §L.1`'s `relation-lookup`/`relation-hydrate-expand` rows), while PathRAG's hl arm additionally hosts a pairwise-DFS-then-flow-weighted-scoring step with no `## §L.1` counterpart at all. This is the fork's actual delta node, and it is cut fresh here, not inherited. [code-verified]

**Carry over.** The keyword-extraction and answer-generation prompt templates (`PROMPTS["keywords_extraction"]`, `PROMPTS["rag_response"]`, `prompt.py`) — read directly from PathRAG's own `prompt.py` rather than assumed identical to LightRAG's from the survey's own "same prompt as LightRAG" note, and found to differ from LightRAG's in edit distance but not in role or shape (both are a JSON-keyword-extraction prompt and a context-to-answer prompt), so `carry over` still applies, evidenced fresh rather than by citation; and the flow-decay path-scoring formula itself (`bfs_weighted_paths`'s edge-weight accumulation, `edge_weights[(node, neighbor)] += 1/len(follow_dict[node])` decayed by `alpha` and cut by `threshold`, `operate.py:1054-1106`) as the `path-enumerator` node's own internal algorithmic core — PathRAG's carry-over item in this bucket is a different algorithmic formula from `GR-1`'s own carry-over item (the round-robin interleave/dedup logic), not the same one restated, exactly as the "same class, still two rows" rule already anticipates at the class level and this entry now confirms at the bucket level. [code-verified]

**The fork-citation question, answered by this exercise rather than assumed.** `### §C.2` as written before this dry-run gives a porter no instruction for what to do when a candidate boundary's code is byte-identical (or near-identical) to a component already dissected and bucketed in an existing `## §E` entry — it states only the re-run semantics for the *same* system changing over time (`### §C.1`'s own "Re-run semantics" paragraph), never the *different-system-shares-code* case D-13 named this dry-run to test. Running the exercise anyway (never citing `GR-1`, deriving every bucket fresh from PathRAG's own code) surfaced the answer directly: the four buckets do **not** track cleanly onto "shared code carries the parent's bucket" — PathRAG's discard bucket is nearly empty where `GR-1`'s is not, precisely because the fork diverged there even though it shares the replace and (partially) the recut and carry-over buckets' code. **What class a fork lands in when most of its components carry over from an existing port rather than from the foreign system directly:** the class is computed exactly as for any other system — from the fork's *own* bucket dominance, counted over its *own* component set, never inherited from the cited port's class. A citation (where the repair below licenses one) supplies evidence for one component's bucket, not a shortcut past deriving which bucket dominates. This is `§D-2`, registered below, with its repair landing in `### §C.2` as a new paragraph naming the citation rule the exercise needed and did not have. [inference] [CONTEXT D-13]

### Step 3 — Code-shape check (`### §C.3`)

Applying `### §C.3`'s four properties to `kg_query` (`operate.py:460-587`) and `_build_query_context` (`operate.py:588-676`):

1. **Injected primitives.** `use_model_func = global_config["llm_model_func"]` (`operate.py:472`) — a bare dict-key lookup with no `partial`/priority wrapping at all, an even less structured form of the same violation `### §C.3` clause 1 already names against LightRAG's `partial(global_config["role_llm_funcs"]["query"], ...)`: no declared client flag, no role-separation guarantee whatsoever (PathRAG has exactly one LLM role, so the `role_llm_funcs`-style separation `## §14.3`'s D12 repair found insufficient for LightRAG does not even arise here — a simpler but not more conformant case). What changes on recut is identical to what `### §C.3` clause 1 already states for LightRAG. No new defect — the existing clause already covers a dict-key-retrieved callable in general, whether or not it is further wrapped in `partial`. [code-verified] [inference]
2. **Plain-data boundaries.** `kg_query`'s return type is bare `str` (`operate.py:460`, no `QueryResult`-style dataclass at all — a step *below* LightRAG's own non-conformant `QueryResult | None`, which at least declared a typed shape even if one field defeated `## §13.2`'s discriminator requirement) — an even plainer instance of the same violation `### §C.3` clause 2 already names, no new defect. **A second, genuinely new instance:** `_build_query_context`'s local/hl split communicates across its two conceptual halves via `query_param.mode`, a shared **mutable argument object** one half rewrites in place (`query_param.mode = "global"` / `query_param.mode = "local"`, `operate.py:604,620,650`) and the join step (`combine_contexts`, called only `if query_param.mode == "hybrid"`, `operate.py:653`) reads back — an implicit side channel between two operations that `### §C.3` clause 2's own violation list (a live handle, a closure, an untyped blob) does not name, because none of those three categories describes a shared object one step writes and a later step reads as a control signal. This is `§D-3`, registered below. [code-verified]
3. **Declared effects.** The response-cache read/write (`handle_cache`/`save_to_cache`) is the same fallback-reachable effect `### §C.3` clause 3 already names for LightRAG, confirmed present in PathRAG's own `kg_query` at `operate.py:472-474,571-580`. No new defect. [code-verified]
4. **No direct store handles.** `kg_query`'s signature (`operate.py:460-468`) carries the identical shape `### §C.3` clause 4 already names as the violation: `knowledge_graph_inst: BaseGraphStorage`, `entities_vdb`/`relationships_vdb: BaseVectorStorage`, `text_chunks_db`/`hashing_kv: BaseKVStorage` — four live storage-backend parameters, not machine-mediated reads. No new defect; the existing clause already covers this shape generally, confirmed here in a second, independently-read codebase. [code-verified]

**Evidence.** File `PathRAG/operate.py` (clone `BUPT-GAMMA/PathRAG`, commit `32567bfc93605b8393996d5fa9ccdc0edbb865b2`): `kg_query` (`:460-587`), `_build_query_context` (`:588-676`), `_get_node_data` (`:686-752`), `_find_most_related_edges_from_entities3` (`:1107-1238`), `find_paths_and_edges_with_stats` (`:1014-1053`), `bfs_weighted_paths` (`:1054-1106`); `PathRAG.py`'s `aquery`/`query`/`ainsert` (`:497-522,273-343`); `base.py`'s `QueryParam` (`:17-27`); `prompt.py`'s `PROMPTS` dict. Every claim above about what the code does is `[code-verified]` and cites function and line; every claim about a recut form is `[inference]`. [code-verified] [inference]

### Step 4 — Exit test on paper (`### §C.4`)

Stating what PathRAG's parity claim would require, nothing run: `bundle@v` and tier are the porter's to name at run time, per `### §C.4`'s own "named here, not designed here" fence — this dry-run does not name a real bundle, since none has been built (Phase 5's own scope). The baseline arm is PathRAG itself (the fork, not its LightRAG parent — `### §C.4`'s "the original, unported system" reads as the system actually being ported, and nothing in `### §C.4` or elsewhere suggests parity is ever measured against an ancestor rather than the ported system itself; this reading needed no repair, since the clause is unambiguous once applied), admitted `opaque` under `## §8`'s eleven conditions exactly as `CA-2`/`MC-1`/`MC-2` already are. The A/A band and delta statistic follow `## §5`'s calibration mechanics by name only, per the RIG-01..07 scope fence `### §C.4` already states. **`### §C.4` was sufficient for this step; no defect found here.** A dry-run is not required to find a defect at every step to be honest — three findings elsewhere (`§D-1` through `§D-3`) are the result of applying the clause literally, not of manufacturing gaps to fill a quota, and this step is the demonstration that the exercise did not do the latter. [inference] [CONTRACT §8] [CONTEXT D-14]

### Dry-run findings and repairs

**§D-1 — the machine-owned-machinery test does not distinguish intra-node from inter-node concurrency.** Found applying `### §C.1` step 1 to PathRAG's own `asyncio.gather()` fan-outs (Step 1a above). The discard bucket's "scheduling/concurrency" bullet names a job `## §9` already owns "regardless of how elaborate the foreign implementation is," worded broadly enough that a porter could read PathRAG's internal fan-out over already-selected nodes — concurrent calls that all fall under one already-declared effect (`reads_graph`/`reads_vector`), for a bounded candidate set the node itself selected — as the same kind of thing as `GR-1`'s cross-stage priority-scheduling harness, and discard it. `## §R N11` already states the contract itself has no declared home for intra-node concurrency in `## §9`'s budget shape or `## §1`'s edge-semantics clause; this finding is `### §C.1`'s own separate, narrower problem — the dissection procedure's *step 1 test* offers no way to route the ambiguity even provisionally, forcing a porter to either guess or stall. **Repair, landed in `### §C.1` step 1** (general, not PathRAG-specific): a node's own internal fan-out over calls that all exercise one already-declared effect, for a candidate set the node itself already selected, stays inside that node as ordinary internal concurrency and is never itself a discard-bucket candidate; the discard bucket's "scheduling/concurrency" bullet names cross-node, cross-stage orchestration only — a scheduler deciding what runs before what *across* nodes, the shape `GR-1`'s worked citation actually shows. This repair unblocks step 1's own test; it does not resolve `## §R N11`'s separate, still-open question of whether `## §9`'s budget shape needs a declared home for intra-node concurrency at all, which remains deferred. [PARTS-07 §D-1] [CONTEXT D-13] [PARTS §R N11]

**§D-2 — no citation rule for a component shared with an already-ported different system (the fork case, D-13's predicted finding).** Found applying `### §C.2` to PathRAG's own components in Step 2 above. `### §C.1`'s "Re-run semantics" paragraph states what happens when the *same* system's dissection is re-run after an upstream change; nothing in `### §C.1` or `### §C.2` states what a porter does when dissecting a *different* system whose code is byte-identical (or near-identical) to a component an existing `## §E` entry already bucketed — a genuine gap, not an oversight this exercise merely re-confirms, since deriving PathRAG's buckets fresh (as the discipline this plan sets requires) surfaced a materially different discard-bucket population from `GR-1`'s despite both systems sharing most of their other bucket content. A porter without this repair has two bad options: re-derive everything from zero even where code is provably unchanged (wasteful, and risks a transcription of the *wrong* prior derivation if done carelessly), or cite the parent's bucket assignment wholesale (as this exercise's own near-miss shows, wrong for the discard bucket specifically). **Repair, landed in `### §C.2`** (general): where a candidate boundary's own code is byte-identical, or differs only in a mechanically negligible way, to a component already dissected and bucketed in an existing `## §E` entry, the porter MAY cite that entry's bucket assignment for the identical component in place of re-deriving it, provided the citation names the existing entry and the specific component, and the porter has actually verified byte-identity or negligible-diff status rather than assumed it from family resemblance — a component that has diverged even slightly is dissected fresh, in full, per `### §C.1`'s ordinary procedure. **The port-cost class question, answered directly:** a fork's port-cost class is never inherited from a cited port's class; it is computed exactly as for any other system, from the fork's own four-bucket dominance over its own component set — a cited-and-verified component still occupies whichever one of the four buckets it was originally found in, it does not create a fifth "inherited" category, and the class calculation counts it there. [PARTS-07 §D-2] [CONTEXT D-13]

**§D-3 — the plain-data-boundaries property does not name a shared mutable argument used as an implicit side channel.** Found applying `### §C.3` clause 2 to `_build_query_context`'s `query_param.mode` reassignment (Step 3 above). Clause 2's violation list — a live iterator handle, a closure, an untyped dict blob — describes three ways a *return value* can fail to be plain typed data, but PathRAG's actual violation is a different shape: a parameter object mutated by one step and read by a later step as a control signal, which is a value crossing between two operations (exactly `### §C.1` step 2's own "a value crosses between two operations" enumeration point) that never appears on any socket at all, because it travels through a shared mutable argument rather than through a `deps` edge or a return value either. **Repair, landed in `### §C.3` clause 2** (general): a shared mutable argument object written by one candidate operation and read by a later one as an implicit signal is exactly the same category of forbidden crossing as a live handle, since neither travels as plain typed data across a declared socket; on recut, the signal becomes an explicit typed field on the `deps` edge between the two resulting nodes (or, where the two operations fuse into one node under `## §19.4`, the signal simply disappears as an internal local variable, no longer crossing anything). [PARTS-07 §D-3]

### Verdict

**A developer could not have ported PathRAG from `### §C.1`–`### §C.4` alone, unrepaired.** Three points required knowledge the protocol text did not state — one dissection-procedure ambiguity that would have risked a wrong discard-bucket call (`§D-1`), one genuine gap in exactly the place D-13 predicted, the fork-citation rule (`§D-2`), and one code-shape violation form the target-shape property's own list did not enumerate (`§D-3`). All three are now repaired in place in `### §C.1`–`### §C.3`, stated generally rather than narrowed to PathRAG, and registered at `## §R P1`–`P3`. **The fork case is answered:** `### §C.2` now states the citation rule a porter needs to reuse a shared component's bucket assignment safely, and states plainly that a fork's port-cost class is computed from its own bucket dominance, never inherited from a cited port's class — the near-miss this exercise itself produced (PathRAG's discard bucket reading nearly empty against `GR-1`'s full one, despite heavy code sharing elsewhere) is exactly the case a blind citation rule would have gotten wrong, and the repair's own verification requirement exists because of it. **This is the expected outcome of a dry-run run honestly, not a failure of the protocol or of this plan:** `### §C.1`–`### §C.4` reached full depth from two worked instances (`GR-1`, `GR-2`) and sixty-plus survey-level breadth entries; PathRAG is the first system the protocol text had to serve *without* the author who wrote it also writing the entry, and three real gaps surfaced under that condition. A dry-run reporting zero insufficiencies here would have been the suspicious result — either the protocol was already complete against a case it had never actually been tested against, or the discipline of deriving fresh rather than citing had quietly slipped. Four of `### §C`'s five procedural steps (`### §C.1` steps 1-3, `### §C.2`, `### §C.3`) needed a repair; the remaining two (`### §C.1` step 4's entry-rung decision, `### §C.4`'s exit test) did not, and are reported clean rather than silently passed over. [inference] [CONTEXT D-13] [PARTS-07]

Three register rules, carried forward from `PARTS.md ## §R` unchanged in force, because they are what `catalog-check.sh` enforces mechanically, not merely aspirational text:

- **One row per defect, keyed by defect id.** Two defects surfaced by the same forcing entry get two rows; two defects landing on the same `CONTRACT.md` clause get two rows.
- **Row order.** Rows are listed ascending by defect id within their id family; families in the order they are introduced (this seed carries only the `N`-family rows `PARTS.md` hands forward).
- **No empty cells.** `Forcing entry` is always a named exercise or entry. `ANATOMY re-projection` is a list of `## §D` entry ids or the explicit value `none — <reason>` where a repair genuinely re-projects nothing.

## Appendix A — Frozen roster

**Satisfies:** PARTS-06

**The inclusion rule (D-10), stated so a reader can reproduce the roster from the sources without this document:**

1. Every numbered system in each of the four modality-pass documents (`MODALITIES-GRAPH.md`, `MODALITIES-VECTOR-TREE.md`, `MODALITIES-AGENTIC.md`, `MODALITIES-CODE-AND-ALIEN.md`) is a roster item, keyed `GR-n` / `VT-n` / `AG-n` / `CA-n` by its own section number.
2. postgres-mcp and supabase-mcp are roster items `MC-1` and `MC-2`, admitted because PARTS-06 names MCP-served query tools explicitly and `CONTRACT §17` already settles their entry path.
3. The vector-only RAG baseline is `VT-1`, already covered by limb 1 and named here because PARTS-06 names it explicitly.
4. Every N-id in `GAP-SWEEP.md ## 2`'s five verdict tables is a roster item keyed `GS-Nn`, **except** where an N-id names a system already entered from a modality pass or where two N-ids name one type the gap sweep itself declares once — in which case the N-id is recorded in the Roster-decision cell of the item that absorbs it, and gets no separate entry (D-09's avoid-near-duplicate-rows rule). All 45 N-ids in `GAP-SWEEP.md ## 2` name types absent from the four modality-pass documents, and each carries its own distinct type name even where `## 3`'s delta list groups several ids under one shared new-capability note. **`N37`/`N38`/`N39` are the one absorption case this roster exercises** (corrected in place at `04-05`, superseding this rule's own earlier worked example, which had stated the opposite before any plan actually wrote these entries): the three share the `self-ingesting` capability, name three distinct types — chat memory, streaming, recommendation — and each still gets its own roster row and its own `## §T`/`## §E` entry per `D-03`'s roster-to-entry crossref requirement, but only `GS-N37` (the lowest N-id) carries the substantive nine-field content; `GS-N38` and `GS-N39` are thin cross-reference entries citing it field by field, with their own Roster-decision cell naming the absorption explicitly. [docs-verified] [inference] [CONTEXT D-10] [PARTS-06 GS-N37]

**The converse, stated because it is the honest half.** Two roster items whose classification fields would read identically still get two entries — identical classification is a finding, not a merge licence, exactly as `PARTS.md ## §P`'s "same class, still two rows" rule already holds for the class label. [docs-verified] [PARTS §P]

**The amendment rule.** An addition to the roster after this freeze carries a stated reason recorded as a `## §R` row. [inference] [CONTEXT D-10]

## Closing sweep — Task 3, Step 2

Four checks, matching the four areas this plan's own instructions name, run over the whole document rather than per-section, with each result stated rather than assumed.

Every `Port cost class:` value and every `## §T` port-cost cell is one of the four named classes (`rehost`/`rewire`/`recut`/`rebuild`), mechanically re-proven by `catalog-check.sh` check 9. Every `Entry path:` value used is one of the four `## Conventions` values or the `out of scope — CONTRACT §N` form; no fifth value or paraphrase found. No drift. [docs-verified]

**Cross-entry agreement.** Five pairs checked, each stated because it was required to agree during the build: `CA-5` and `GS-N19` on the formal-query-language-versus-`§17` question — both cite `§15.1`'s Branch 1 test and reach opposite, mutually consistent verdicts for the two concrete cases (a foreign RDBMS fails Branch 1; a query surface over the graph store's own schema passes it), neither restating the other's evidence. `MC-1`/`MC-2` and `CA-5` on the relational-system-of-record question — `CA-5`'s own entry states explicitly that it and the two MCP entries "apply the identical §15.2/§17 disposition to two different concretes," and `MC-1`/`MC-2`'s own entry paths (`external-tool hosted opaque, §17; §15 Branch 4 redirect`) match that disposition. `GS-N20` and `GS-N43` on checkpoint-bearing version identity — `GS-N20`'s entry resolves the CAP-table's `N20`/`N43` grouping via `§0`/`§1`, and `GS-N43`'s own entry cross-references that resolution rather than re-deciding it; both directions confirmed present. `GR-1` and `GR-5` on the fork relationship — **one gap found and fixed in place**: `GR-5`'s own entry (written in this plan) checked itself against `GR-1` at every bucket, but `GR-1`'s entry (written in plan 04-01, before `GR-5` existed) carried no reciprocal reference. Repaired by adding a short cross-reference note to `GR-1`'s `Evidence:` field naming the fork relationship and the discard-bucket divergence, closing the pair from both sides. `VT-1`, `CA-6` and `GS-N22` on the ratchet extremes — all three state `none` in their `Advantages decomposition would earn:` field with the identical "already at the floor"/"already at the two-node floor" framing; no divergence.
